ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e4621	Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Mr President. In the two and a half years since the formation of the Austrian Federal Government, particularly in the social sector and in the apostrophic areas of family support, the Austrian government has made a great deal of progress. The childcare allowance mentioned by Mr Klubobmann Westenthaler is a Europe-wide recognised measure which is not only close to the intentions of women. We know from surveys that more than 87 percent of women wish to stay at home with their children for at least one year, and that more than 70 percent of women want to return to work at least after the third year of childcare. I think that after the introduction of the childcare allowance, the international benchmarks are also of great importance. We currently have 73.4% of the highest value in the second Republic in Austria as regards the female employment rate, which is between 20 and 45 years of age. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] With regard to child care, the European Union admits to us that it is an important means of combating poverty in Austria. Despite the public debate in Austria, we have increased the share of social expenditure in this state from 28.7 percent of gross domestic product in 1999 to 30.2 percent. The European average is 27.6 percent. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I can point out that social protection expenditure per capita and purchasing power parity in Austria amount to €6 716, on European average €5 793. The employment rate of 68.2 percent is 5 percent above the EU average rate of 63.2 percent. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] With regard to the unemployment rate, our 3.7 percent is compared with 8.2 percent of the EU; for men, 3.2% are compared with 7.0%, and for women 4.4% compared with 9.7%. We are third in the European Union of all 15 European countries. We are still the country with the lowest youth unemployment. We have been able to significantly reduce the long-term unemployment rate for men and women over the last 12 months. We were able to reduce the risk of poverty in Austria. The European Union also confirms that the risk of poverty in Austria is particularly low. The gap between the richest and the poorest is the lowest in the EU in Austria. I therefore believe that the social balance of this federal government can be seen. Ladies and gentlemen! If we look at the history of "handling" we can see that it was first introduced in 1985 by the Liberal Group in the Chamber of Workers in Salzburg and finally, in 1990 by Mr Dolinschek in today's form in the National Council. After 17 years of discussion, a settlement model could be implemented here, which will not only enable almost 100 percent of Austrian employees to claim compensation, but can also be an important instrument of tax exemption for unabsorbed treatment and the construction of a future pension component, provided it is used responsibly by the employees to improve and significantly build up the Rudimentary Second Pillar of Pension Provision in Austria. We agreed with the national social partners a year ago to make an information campaign under the title "Provisioned". I think that, when we look at the past in which the purchase of social security rights has affected women, especially those in a divorce situation, we must be of this opinion – in the context of the "reparation new" also the young generation between 20 and 35 years of age cannot draw attention early enough to the fact that the new instrument of handling will now not only allow 100 percent of workers the proven form of processing, but also that, regardless of the course of life, there is also the possibility of applying an additional pension free of tax. I think it is an important contribution to reserve this part of the tax revenue for the second pension column in the future if people now choose it on the basis of the settlement model. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] We have, as promised, evaluated the child care allowance. We know from the evaluation of the childcare allowance in the introduction communities that a high proportion of the childcare allowance, i.e. more than 60 percent, goes into direct consumption and that the local care area is particularly beneficial. Austria will be discussing in the municipalities and cities how we can improve the rural structure in Austria in terms of local services. With the introduction of the childcare allowance, we have made an important contribution to the fact that in the future, the Austrian villages and communities will receive a better infrastructure in the near-care sector than was previously expected. Ladies and gentlemen! On tax reform, I must say, in my view, as a social minister, that a tax reform is essential, and that is why it is necessary to relieve the beneficiaries of the smallest, the lowest income in this Republic. Indeed, it is unmistakable that income, which is just above the poverty limit for married couples mentioned in the 1998 poverty report, is still subject to tax liability. It is therefore particularly important for these levels of income in the current situation, as is contained in the Government Declaration, to implement a corresponding tax reform, tax relief. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] There are no financial experts to be taken seriously in this Republic, which does not prove that, with a certain delay effect of three to four months, a large part of this regulatory effect is going into direct consumption and will thus lead to an increase in the demand for the internal market in Austria, which will further boost the now increasing economy. Ladies and gentlemen! I therefore believe that the tax reform is also an important contribution to strengthening the now increasing economy in the internal market demand and thus the Austrian economy in order to improve the employment situation in Austria in order to return to those unemployment figures that we in Austria saw in 2000 and 2001 before the disaster in the 11th century. ==References====External links== The objective of each responsible social politician must be to push down the unemployment rate. I think, ladies and gentlemen, that the accompanying measures we have taken up to now are also important in order to establish new businesses and new businesses in Austria. [[Abg. Silhavy: Which one?]] The one with the 60. ASVG-Novelle's planned discharge in the KV for new business start-ups and for weak businesses has the purpose of keeping them competitive and is an important contribution to attracting employment and business. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! However, it is not only a question of achieving a reduction in income up to €3 000 gross per month, but also of implementing a fair collective reform and the 13th. To recover revenue tax prepayment from the world. It is probably also important for the Austrian public to learn that at the date of the 8th anniversary. July, i.e. yesterday, 25 513 applications for child care are available. [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] The Austrian social system is based on the generation contract. Today, we only have a birth rate of just under 76,000 births, so I am pleased that since April of this year it has been detectable that the birth rate is increasing, for example in Styria by 13.1 percent, in Carinthia by 8.3 percent, and in Vorarlberg by 10.7 percent. I mention these examples because there is a new generation that we will need in order to be able to comply with the generation agreement. I therefore think, ladies and gentlemen, that this Federal Government has proved that it is making "social policy with heart and mind" for the entire population.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e7350	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! We are now dealing with this amendment to the Foreign Law, the Asylum Act and the Foreign Employment Act. It may already be that many behind these three novels suspect only very certain groups of people, but in reality these three legislative novels affect all the people living in the country, including those who want to work on our borders in our country. The goal that you hear time and again is: Integration before re-entry, full employment, the increase in the employment rate. – Ladies and gentlemen, very much! This has also been demonstrated in various contributions in the previous debate. In the last five years, ladies and gentlemen of the coalition parties, employment has increased in Austria, not even since this new federal government was established. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] That is why we should not hide the fact that since May of the previous year, with regard to employment, Mr Westenthaler, we have been increasingly removing ourselves from the goal of full employment. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: We have a foreign package! I'm afraid you have the wrong talk with me!]] And this is not from the source of the trade union federation, but the source is the economic report of the Austrian Federal Government 2002. We are becoming more and more rid of the goal of full employment. According to the latest board advice, the labour market service lacks €830 million for the 2002 budget. You will still be able to pursue this, Mr Westenthaler, because it shows above all that there has to be a policy different from that you always want to communicate with, that you are there for the little man. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: You're on the wrong topic!]] But with the rules in the Foreign Employment Act, on the question of integration, you really open the labour market. This is your policy, which contradicts exactly what you say otherwise, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Now we are on the subject!]] What are the main effects of this novel from our point of view? – It is not about integration in your proposals, but about the establishment of new precarious working conditions. It is not about full employment, but through the new seasonal model, there is a corresponding irritation on the labour market for all sectors. You see that in your proposals. It is not an extension with a win-win situation, but the donor scheme you are planning is partly driving into the illegality and, above all, in the housing market, into a situation that we all cannot want if we consider it for the future. Not to mention the joint venture regulation relating to training, which does not provide for any regulation of actual work and which may take place outside any legal and labour legislation. Let's see that in detail! What you are planning on the seasonal arrangement can also be referred to as creative accounting, similar to what you are planning in financing health insurance, ladies and gentlemen. We should not forget that, for example, in the 2001 labour market report – your own report, Mr. Federal Minister, no one else! – it is noted that the unemployment rate has increased by 5.7 percent in seasonal kidneys and that, regrettably, the working groups outside the seasonal arrangements have also experienced an increase in the unemployment rate, but a lower, namely, only 4.7 percent. So one percentage point more unemployment in seasonal kidneys! If seasonal eggs are restricted to agriculture and tourism today, I must say that we are facing the situation that 31 700 employment permits have been granted in 1999 and 4 400 have been employed on a monthly basis, but 52 000 employment permits have already been granted in 2001 and 9 300 have been employed on a monthly basis, Minister of State! [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: What about the annual average? How many were the annual averages?]] According to labour market statistics B 750 9 300 has been employed each month, instead of the 8,000 that you always mention. If you count 9 300 times 12, then you'll find that this isn't right. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: That's why we make the law!]] Despite these suggestions, Mr Westenthaler, despite the development you are seeing, you are taking a path where you will no longer be able to follow a country throughout Europe. Even Switzerland, which has long enough experience with seasonal eggs and knows how it works, has been since 1. In July, you decided to withdraw from the arrangement you provided for. They force an exchange procedure between workers. Whoever complains, flies, who complains, is outside. They do not want any orderly employment, even for seasonals, but for the availability of workers, even in labour chain contracts. If they're here for twelve months, they'll have to take a two-month break, then they can get busy again. – This is not the policy we are concerned with! We want permanent jobs, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] If I first spoke of creative accounting, then I have to say, please look at your own design. What does "weighted average" mean? [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Read more! Read next sentence! I'll explain that to you!]] Sage You know what that is! You're the next speaker. You will not be able to provide us with the proof that you want to have orderly conditions, but with this novelle It opens the labour market not only to agriculture, tourism, but to all seasonals and thus to a corresponding deregulation of working conditions. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Read the law! You haven't even read the law!]] With your model you actually go back to the rotation model of the 1960s. Neither in Switzerland nor in the European Union is this model followed. Therefore, I believe that another way would be the right one: ordinary working conditions also for seasonal workers, and not the labour law under the custodial of ownership, which you imagine here. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Federal Minister Dr. Bartenstein: Right of establishment!]] Ladies and gentlemen! We want clear conditions, Mr. Federal Minister, and not those regulations that you yourself are here... – Let's change the law in a proper way, but not in the direction that you say seasonal unemployment is rising. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Why have you approved it in recent years?]] [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: We do this today!]] They say seasonal unemployment is rising. Your alternative is: Seasonal workers for all sectors and not restriction. They are increasing the destabilisation of the labour market and not the security, Mr Westenthaler! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In this context, it also goes further: Under the title "Joint Venture", a new system for foreign workers in our country is proposed for training. Many of you probably know the example of the hen and the pig if you want to describe the joint venture being. A hen comes to the pig and says: Let's make a joint venture! The pig asks: What does that mean to me? That's what the hen says: I'll bring in the egg and you the ham. That's what the pig means: Then I'm dead! That's what the hen says: That's the essence of a joint venture. [[Cheerfulness at the SPÖ.]] The reality you want creates real distress, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. That's not a real joint venture in the positive sense. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] What's your proposal? – A foreign employer concludes a joint venture contract for foreign education in Austria with an Austrian employer. He's got six months to do that. What does he have to do? – There must be a foreign employer, but whether there is an Austrian, or whether it is an Austrian, who is a foreign company, that is another question that the AMS cannot verify on the basis of the circumstances you also provide in this law. The second point envisaged here is: A training programme must be submitted – a training programme to be reviewed by the AMS. For example: 10 hours of theory and 40 hours of practical training on the vehicle. 24-hour drivers, who work in a corresponding volunteer relationship, have only recently met us. That's the practice you define by law. The foreign employer must complete a training programme with the Austrian employer. Six months are planned for this. There is no indication of what the training programme must include, and no indication of how it is verifiable. It is only noted in the explanatory remarks that the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs is preparing an appropriate framework somewhere. But this is not a method of procedure that is shown there. It is assumed that this will somehow take place. – There is no reference to labour and contract law and no reference to remuneration. If you also write in these novels on the Foreign Employment Act that the legal provisions must be complied with and therefore mean the collective agreement, then you forget that in practice there are also overpayments of collective contracts, which are then almost abolished by your regulation, so that in reality a reduction to the collective contract wage takes place. All of these are small pieces of puzzle in relation to the goal you have set in connection with the novel to the Foreign Law, Asylum Law and Foreign Employment Act. That is why I am convinced that our remark is correct. What you are planning here is far from real integration. What you are planning here is far from the common goal of full employment. And what you are planning here is far away from increasing the employment rate of the people in the country, regardless of whether foreigners or foreigners. We have not been separated, but we are concerned that we have a qualified labour market for the future, and not employment relations, as we have always had to find them in recent times. What we want is not a destabilisation of the working world. With your seasonal arrangement, with your joint venture regulations and partly also with the regulations on the weekly commuters, you will create a further destabilisation of the working world and, above all, employment conditions which you cannot really want, especially when I remember what you, Mr Schweitzer, said here in March in the context of a current hour. Look Your own proposal! Read it through, and then compare it to the real world of work! Then you will find that you may have gone into some of the economic interests of the glue, but certainly do not pursue the goal of orderly and secure working conditions on socially fair conditions. I'm sure that's not what this novelle does. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I repeat: Integration before re-entry is not at all addressed in this novel. These are not measures to integrate foreign labour. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: German courses!]] Remember that the economy said about two years ago: We need 50 000 new specialists in the IT sector. There is today the highest unemployment. [[Abg. May. Schweitzer: They wanted the SPÖ, we fought against it!]] If we had followed these proposals then this would clearly have been negative. What we want is fair working and living conditions for all in the country. But why are you making this novel, Mr Schweitzer? – This novelle favours this process and does not reduce negative decisions. We don't want an exchange of labour. Take the example of Switzerland or, if you do not want to use Switzerland, take the Wifo, which in a report on this law even states that these measures will be used to promote the exchange of labour – not to mention the question of the skills development of this labour force. What we want is to exploit the domestic potential, i.e. the Austrian, the foreigner in the country. We have to put in place qualification measures and not reduce or eliminate the AMS resources. We have to deal with the question of older workers and should not replace them with seasonal workers in all sectors. We have to take the fight against black entrepreneurs seriously and should not just handcuff foreign workers who are being picked up. Put yourself in the situation of a seasonal worker who complains because he has no proper working conditions! He flies out, goes abroad – he can't stay at home anymore because he's out of work – and then has to file a complaint against the employer from somewhere. These are things I say: This cannot be done if we want real integration, if we also take foreigners' employment seriously! Look at the law! You may still have the opportunity to make changes.

Your conclusions will and will discuss this with us publicly. The 2002 migration report, commissioned by the European Union and published in Germany by the two experts Klaus Bade and Rainer Münz, includes a preface to the German President, in which he writes that these developments must be taken into account. If you look at these developments, you have to agree with us when we say that there are worrying developments in the labour market. This report states – with a careful estimate – that: The underlying and partly very optimistic assumptions of the model are responsible for the figures. – That is to say, you are optimistic about lower numbers. The experts are telling us that immigration from the Central and Eastern European countries of 1998 – at that time it was 103 000 – will more than quadruple by 2030. We must consider this development. The experts of the "ifo" – I quote literally the "ifo" – that is the institution that has worked out this study – see not only the wage difference as an important cause of migration, but also the more attractive social security systems in Western Europe. The experts of the "ifo" therefore proposed that migration should not be limited by restrictions on free movement, but that immigrants should adapt their rights to social security systems to the level of the respective countries of origin. It must be possible to quote this and bring it into the political debate – that is, to try to bring in a new idea, namely that we should try not to give any better in the social system in the future, even in the social system, but rather to orient ourselves to the social system of the countries of origin, so that a substantial reason for migration is put forward – and then to discuss it, even if the experts bring it in, if this is a highly official study commissioned by the European Union. We will take these figures very seriously because we do not want the Austrian labour market to be at risk by further or new immigration and because we have to start at the roots. The root of immigration, the migration of people from the East to us lies on the one hand at the wage level and on the other hand in the good, narrow Austrian social system. That is why most of the immigrants also indicate the objective of Austria. That is why we need to consider new measures. I consider the idea of whether in the future we will have to pay new immigrants, who will also come to Austria due to the EU enlargement, the same social benefits – I mean transfers and no insurance benefits – and also finance them as before. I think we have to consider whether it should actually be so, or whether we should take the experts' reminders seriously. Today's law makes us clear: Austria is not an immigration country, nor will it become one. We'll take care of it!

The 2001 census, of which the results are now published gradually, clearly states that 730 239 foreigners lived in Austria on the date of the 2001 census. In this number, however, EU citizens are already included, who, as we know, are equal to the Austrians in social and political terms. Except for voting rights to general representatives, such as the National Council and the Federal President, EU citizens are allowed to share what Austrians are allowed to do. It is therefore no longer permitted to call it foreigners. Thus, some 630 000 people are so-called third-country nationals living in Austria. So that's far from a million. The mastery of basic accounting is also an art that one should be able to do in politics. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The workplace is the main integration rail for immigrants. Prinzhorn shall take over the chairmanship.]] Integration is a mutual process, and integration cannot be reduced how this Bun-des-Government, with its forced integration agreement, wants to make us credible through this form of octroying, the imposition of language courses, to the mere learning of the language. Integration is a process on both sides. Language is important. I consider the language of the new home country to be an invaluablely important and valuable asset in the integration process. That is why it is also so important to us that policy offers and creates incentives. The greater the respect for the original language of immigrants, the greater the support, the greater the success of language learning, the more that scientists confirm, that is quite clear, no one puts it in apprehension – except the Austrian Federal Government with this integration package. The fact that immigrants who live permanently in Austria, have to learn German and above all want to learn German has often been demonstrated in the past. But the conditions for successful language acquisition cannot be called punishment, fine, disfellowshipping or abduction from that country. If someone believes that one creates motivation, impetus or incentive to learn the language with the threat of the expulsion or deportation from Austria, then he is really on the absolute path of wood. We must face the fact that integration and integration policies mean, on the one hand, to put in place measures that actually enable integration for those who are present, and on the other hand to formulate immigration provisions, immigration schemes that are in fact in line with the needs of the Austrian population, the Austrian economy and the future immigrants, and are in line with those provisions which become standard in Europe through EU directives. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] This reorganisation is based on very clear principles that Klubobmann Khol has set out here in an impressive way. One of the most important innovations is finally the harmonisation of the right of foreigners to employment with the right of foreigners. At the same time, this means a clear definition of key workers and key forces. This is a major procedural simplification. This means for the first time in the history of Austrian foreign law integration agreements and integration promotion for foreign citizens in Austria. This also means simplification for certain groups, and this means finally preventing abuse. I would like to address some of these points in detail. First of all: There will be a substantial procedural simplification through this law: an authority, a decision, an appeal, and thus we will achieve a substantial acceleration from currently months of waiting time to a few weeks. Secondly, For the first time – here, the government programme is being implemented to the point and the point – a federal government is starting the integration of foreign citizens and implementing the principle of integration before new entry. We are now doing this in a very clear and clear way – whether through the consultation and support of immigrants, whether through training measures for foreign citizens, whether through specific offers for migrants through appropriate courses for women, through special offers for childcare and through the integration agreement, which of course may provide a basic course, the dissemination of basic knowledge, basic knowledge of German language, but also the closeness of everyday life – administrative processes, citizenship and the communication of the basic values of the European community of values. But we are not a typical country of immigration. Typical immigration countries are countries such as the USA, Canada and Australia. We're very different here, and that's a big difference. We are interested in regulated immigration then when the Austrian labour market endures, when it endures Austrian economic power and when it bears Austrian social reality. It is our task – both in the Ministry of the Interior and in the other ministries, as well as in the Parliament – to meet this fundamental requirement of Austrian society. The new regulation will strengthen Austria's economic location. The criticism of the exploitation of foreign seasonal eggs goes into emptiness. We do not make a humane policy, Mr Parnigoni, as you have put us here today. There is no wage dumping! The seasonal kidneys are paid according to the collective contract and treated as domestics. So no mention of wage slavery, as was claimed here. The new regulation alters the existing regulatory practice, which has not provided sufficient quotas for workers in peak periods. This new system is also very important in terms of EU enlargement. In order to maintain competitiveness, we need these harvest helpers. And the safeguarding of the domestics Harvest to supply the Austrian population with the top products of domestic agriculture must be guaranteed! - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e13277	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. Federal Minister! I'm trying to get the whole debate a little bit too messy again. The amendment of the Assembly Law, which we will adopt today, has become necessary because the willingness to act on behalf of maimed groups has increased enormously during demonstrations in recent years. High costs have been incurred for damage to property as well as for personal injury: Money for which taxpayers, that is, all of us, must come; human suffering and physical pain for which the State and also for us Parliamentary co-responsibility. Freedom of assembly and demonstration in Austria is a valuable social asset. It is important to protect this good responsibly and to preserve it for all people in this country. As a convinced democrat and human rights activist, it is a great personal concern for me to maintain freedom of assembly and to allow all people to use their democratic right to democracy without fear of violent outrages. ==References====External links== In July, all the experts involved in professional demonstrations took a stand for a disguised ban, because violence is more likely to be based on disguised people. [[Abg. Perfuss: It's not true!]] Nevertheless, the principle of proportionality has been taken into account in this amendment, Mrs Colleague. Reduced funds instead of arrest are anchored if you can achieve the same purpose. A new opportunity principle has been created. In the field of punishments, too, the eye has been kept in mind. However, this change of law will satisfy its objective of preventing violent demonstrators and protecting the peaceful demonstrators, but also our executives accordingly.

[[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Elderly structures have been corrected and replaced by modern structures. Staff has not been reduced – this is also a fact. I must tell colleagues who were not in the committee that the interest of the opposition in questioning the individual experts and ministers was poor. The exact and detailed explanations were not heard and accepted, but they were only met with polemics. I must say, however, that I do not expect anything else from my colleague. I would like to draw on the example of the district courts to explain the effectiveness of the reforms. Ladies and gentlemen! Today's court structure dates back to 1848. At that time, explosive boundaries were based on the fundamental rulers, which made the explosives very heterogeneous. The different sizes of explosives due to the power of the time create great differences in the business and human dosages of the individual courts. For example, the smallest of the 192 district courts is responsible for approximately 4,800 inhabitants, the largest for about 350 000. On average, about 1,300 so-called cases of justice per district court and year, but in 85 district courts less than one civil judgment per week. Further, one will certainly consider which quick tests could still be used. It is important, however, that the entry into the matter has now been carried out and not delayed further. The latter should actually be the meaning of the road traffic regulations. We didn't want to do a drug policy with the StVO. There are approximately 250 substances covered by the Drugs Act. They have different effects, and the conclusion that if such substances are demonstrated so that there would be immediate inability is not sustainable. You know that just as we do, and that has been stressed by the experts over and over again. The introduction of limit values with regard to the ability to drive does not take place in the same way as in the case of alcohol consumption. In contrast, alcohol can be precisely defined by the promille limit and can also measure and determine the indifference or impairment. Not each of the substances illegalised by the Addiction Act leads to an impairment of the ability to travel. A limit value would have to be introduced for each substance, otherwise a positive result could be achieved through legal consumption, such as by eating a poppet. A positive urine or blood test for the first moment says little about the ability to drive. We were against the urinary samples, which have always been so strongly propagated by you at the beginning – let us be glad that this point has gone away! . because we believe that a urinal test would not be aimed at demonstrating the current impairment of the ability to travel, because it only detects the depleting substances of the addiction. Due to the completely different periods of degradation of various drugs in the body, for example, in the case of cannabis, the evidence by urea test is still possible several weeks after consumption, but this does not result in any impairment of the current ability to travel. It is therefore not to be ruled out, in the case of measures against vehicle drivers, that there could be disagreements in the case of longer-term consumption of illegal substances which are demonstrated by the test. How this is handled in practice will still be seen. We will examine how you will explain the rules of implementation then, Minister of State. The blood test is at least suitable to demonstrate a drug use within the last hours. Therefore, it is possible to speak of a support of the perceived inability to drive. In any event, the relative inability to carry out a clinical investigation, to which we attach particular importance, as well as other evidence such as driving errors or signs to the person, symptoms of failure, which can also be detected in the event of alcohol consumption, such as fluctuating gait, blurry speech and so on, is decisive. In the blood test there is no linear link between impairment and blood value; a limit setting is also very, very difficult in this case. Ladies and gentlemen! It can also be unclear what is now considered a refusal and what is not. A written instruction on the consequences of refusal should be made in the sense of legal certainty and civil kindness. It will also be very important for the executives to receive appropriate training and training in this direction, as well as a precise checklist to be applied throughout Austria in order to be able to record an impairment of travel availability on the spot on the basis of the checklist. Therefore, once again: We do not want comprehensive discrimination, but for us the requirement is for us to give this law the consent that traffic-related behaviour is a basic requirement that a clinical investigation be carried out, carried out in Austria by experts, and that blood tests are carried out only in qualified laboratories. All these measures must be said again and again: Neither drugs nor alcohol should be associated with driving a motor vehicle!

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e16211	We're on the 8th. Agenda item. There is no need for oral reporting. We are going into the debate. First speaker is Mrs Binder. Voluntary speaking time limit: 5 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e16241	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It is a fact that starters are above average often involved in road accidents. The present draft law, which amends the driving licence law, takes account of this and provides for the introduction of a multi-phase driving licence. What's the multi-phase driver's license about? On the one hand, it is about reducing the high risk of accident among drivers, which is mainly due to a lack of driving routine and a very high level of risk. This is why a second phase of training is to be introduced. Such a model has already been successfully tested in Finland. This is also the focus of the new provisions on driving training. What is the content of this training, ladies and gentlemen? – In the second phase of training, participants will be shown the risks of driving motor vehicles on the basis of a driving safety training for a period of three to nine months following the acquisition of the driving licence. Secondly, by means of a perfection journey, two to four months and six to twelve months after the acquisition of the steering licence, as well as by means of traffic psychological group discussions, the high level of risk for young drivers is planned. Any additional costs, ladies and gentlemen, could be reduced by a revision of the driving school law. There is a proposal on the part of SPÖ in this respect. Minister! I think it is up to you, in the sense of young people, to take action and act in this regard. The aim is to reduce the number of accidents by completing training and training measures at a time when the driver or driver is still in a phase of the collection of experience. Facts and figures speak a clear language: Any second fatal accident of the 18-20-year-old driver is a single accident. In Finland, the new training content under the multi-phase driver's licence has reduced the number of starting accidents by 50 percent within two years. The economic benefits can also be underestimated: In the event of a 20 percent accident, some €27 million per driver's licence year could be saved. According to a study, 70 percent of Austrians would be in favour of multi-phase training and would choose to do so even if it cost more. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e16392	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! In summary, I would like to say that four objectives are pursued with multi-phase training. These are located in the individual area, in the personal area, but they are also visible in the economic field. I believe that the most important measure – which was already implemented in the last debate contribution – is the reduction of accidents. I think – and this is what the experts tell us – that with the introduction of the "vehicle training new" a 20 percent reduction in the accident rate is also realistic from a relatively short-term perspective. There are experiences from Finland where this multi-phase driving training was initially a model experiment and was then introduced fixed. The figures we know from this country speak for themselves and give the right to the introduction of multi-phase training there. It is asked again and again: Why should we introduce this directly into Austria? – The problem in Austria is impressively shown in a graphic. We are in Europe with those killed in road transport in the group of 18-20-year-olds in the absolute peak field. Let me present a few figures. The measurement is: killed inhabitants of this age group per million inhabitants. Austria is 292.5. Only France is in Europe with 292.7 in an even worse situation than Austria. "Spitzenreiter" – under quotation marks – is respectively the best value the United Kingdom has with 110.5. So we have nearly three times as many killed in road traffic as Great Britain. Why this is so has quite different reasons. First point: In Austria, the experts agree that the introduction of this multi-phase training will achieve tangible success very quickly. That is why we want to do this now, and we want to go through it quickly and quickly, in order to set appropriate standards in the field of road safety in Austria, especially with regard to the age group of 18 to 20 years olds, i.e. with regard to driving licence newcomers, so to speak, the beginners in road traffic. I think that's good and necessary. Second point: In addition to the reduction of accidents, of course – Mr Kukacka has done this briefly – there is a direct link to the economic benefits of the people. The figures, which are also scientifically hardened, speak for themselves. A person killed in an accident causes direct costs and follow-up costs in an order of €908,000, which is at least 12.5 million schilling. If you take the 20 percent reduction in accidents as a basis of assessment as the basis of calculation, then this results in an order of 27 million euros per driver's licence year. So that's building up quite well. Third point: In Austria – in this respect, the Board of Trustees for Transport Safety, ARBÖ and ÖAMTC have conducted several studies – acceptance of these model tests is extremely high. 70 percent of Austrians are for multi-phase training, even if it would cost more. This was so to speak of the return of this survey, which is the famous Yes study. This is, in itself, a very high degree of acceptance of a fundamentally new thing which is not yet known in the public. But I would also like to say the following: As a legislator, we have tried not to let the driver's license become more expensive. We see this under the word "cost neutrality". I think that although the new calculation schedule and the new training plan have reduced theory hours, practical hours have been increased, a shift has occurred. In conjunction with feedback driving and driving safety training, this results in an equal number of hours, ladies and gentlemen. I am also sure that, as soon as the first insurance company in Austria takes off from its own initiative a certain treatment of new drivers' licenses, which they enter into the group of 25-year-old, trained, active road users – there is good evidence for this – will also follow everyone else, because the insurance industry cannot afford to follow a very good example. Although we are still trying to get a grip on some other things, for example as regards competition issues in the field of driving schools, I am quite sure that a much higher level of quality in the field of driving training will be achieved – at the same level, possibly even at lower costs.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e16765	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The accident statistics speak a very clear language: The lion's share of the unhappiness of the vehicle controls is by far the young car drivers up to 24 years. It is mainly male car drivers in this age group who are to be complained of as accident victims. Two main components result in this blatant share in young car drivers: lack of driving routine in combination with high risk willingness. The present application for the introduction of the multi-phase driver's licence will take measures to elucidate these two main accident components. Multi-phase driving licence means several phases of learning: first, an adapted curriculum theoretically and practically, second, a driving safety training and feedback driving to improve the driving routine, and thirdly, traffic psychological group discussions to limit increased risk willingness. The previously possible L17 driving licence has shown that more driving practice reduces the frequency of accidents. The L17 will continue to be maintained and the increased driving practice will also be taken into account in the new multi-phase driving licence. At the same time, the driver's licence will not be expensive, and the insurance premiums for young drivers will be reduced in the long term. It is our duty to do everything possible to reduce the number of victims of accidents in the area of our young people. The multi-phase driving licence is an important step in training our youth not only theoretically but above all practically. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Our youth will be accompanied professionally for a whole year in the phases of learning with the multi-phase licence. Ladies and gentlemen! I am very glad today that this proposal will be adopted by all parties in the sense of the security of our youth.

It is therefore also important that rural areas, particularly in the field of telecommunications, should not be forgotten. Consumer protection is also an important point. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e17821	Mr President. Mr. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The liberalisation of the telecommunications market in Austria has been initiated by the Telecom Act in force since 1997 and has brought about competition in a previously monopolistic market. The economy as a whole has brought them economic benefits. The basis for competition was created by asymmetric regulation. The current major changes in the telecoms market have prompted the European Union to create a new regulatory framework for electronic communications networks and services. This legal framework will take account of the convergence between telecommunications, broadcasting and information technologies. Competition in all market segments is to be re-enhanced while ensuring that the fundamental rights of consumers continue to be protected. The new EU legal framework is designed for new dynamic and largely unpredictable markets, where a much larger number of telecom operators will operate than today. Member States will now be granted a deadline for national implementation by July 2003. In Austria, this issue has already been adopted. Preparations for the recast of the Telekom Act were carried out in the Transport Committee and also in the Subcommittee, while expert opinions were heard. Following a telecom summit, a draft telecommunications law will now be reviewed in mid-July. The new Telecommunications Act is intended to bring more customer-friendliness to mobile communications. With the introduction of the telephone number portability, customers who want to change the operator will be able to carry their telephone number and pre-election in the future. A signal is intended to clarify whether the customer is still on the telephone in its operator network or not. However, the new Telecommunications Act aims to further increase competition in order to increase the transparency of tariffs for consumers. The scheme must also include appropriate framework conditions for the recovery of this sector in Austria. It is therefore important to ensure that there are effective competition rules and that the current crisis in the telecommunications sector can be overcome. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e18844	Mrs. Vice chancellor! Mr President. This deregulation law for the public service is simply and simply a questionable matter. Changes are provided for in the right of employment and remuneration, including the abandonment of forbearance, the elimination of rights of participation and powers, the reorganisation of training for the public service et cetera. It is positive – and that is the only thing to be supported – that double and multiple powers are eliminated by removing powers of participation from the Minister of Finance and the Federal Ministry for Public Performance and Sport. This will be in line with the order contained in the title "Deregulation Act". The other regulations, as I see them, are usually not the case. No deregulation is the planned removal of forbearance if appointment and definition requirements are not met. Rather, this is a clear restriction on the possibility of being incorporated into a public service relationship. It is also questionable whether the resolution of the administrative academy has anything to do with deregulation intentions. In the explanations, you try to make a strong connection by criticizing the administrative academy, accusing its lack of flexibility, administrative overload and so on, but this criticism is not justified, because it is contrary to the Austrian Federal Administration's performance report of October 2001. Editor of this report is your own ministry, Mrs. Minister. This report confirms that it is the best training institution for public servants in the international comparison, and also confirms that no other comparable institution has achieved the efficiency indicators. Since the administrative academy also mediates cross-sectional arteries, this is a common bracket that increases the mobility of federal staff. So if you move basic training completely into the individual units, then this common parentheses is missing, and the mobility of the federal staff is restricted. This is contrary to your interests, Mrs Bundesminister, who is constantly talking about increased mobility in the public sector and who is calling for it. So your words and actions, as usual, do not agree. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Even the argument that there has been too little respect in basic education has not been given, because the administrative academy has always kept close contact with the resorts, and in addition, it would be within the competence of your Federal Ministry to amend the basic training regulations accordingly. Dissolve the administrative academy now, which is absolutely not comprehensible. My group also completely rejects the fact that a subjective training right is a purely service-based training system. What has so far been a subjective training right is from now on an arrangement of training by the service provider. Of course, it must be seen that this is a significant intervention in the career and life planning of the individual, because for the career training is essential. This will result in less training for displeasing officials. But the most politically brilliant and democratically empathetic is the application to the Federal Staff Representative Act by the Deputies Reindl and Baumgartner-Gabitzer, because this request does not mean anything other than the prevention of the personnel representation elections for so long as the term of office of this government continues, although the applicable law would require that staff representation elections be held immediately – and for deep reorganisation reasons. There will probably be a role to play in this – and I am playing on the previous item on the agenda – and the Minister of Sport is certainly aware of this: There is the book by Peter Handke "The fear of the Tormann of the Elfmeter." With regard to the personal representation elections, we will have to say in the alternation of this book title: The fear of the ÖVP from PV elections. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This is not only a question of democracy, it is also constitutional, because there is no factual justification for arbitrarily stopping the election of personnel representatives for so long as the period of office of the government lasts. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-09-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00109_d7e18944	Mrs. Vice chancellor! Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! It is indeed true that this deregulation law achieves a substantial administrative simplification in Austria. I would just like to give you one example: In view of the current legal situation, it is true that if a distributed undertaking, a distributed body for an official has to make a change in the assessment or assignment of the workplace, or if it wishes to make a request to the competent ministry, it is necessary to make a change in the assessment or assignment of the workplace. This request then goes to the Federal Ministry of Public Performance and Sport, and the Ministry then has to submit an application to the Council of Ministers, and there this request requires a unanimous decision. Now this double conductivity or versatility is eliminated. I believe that this is an essential step forward, which also results in cost savings. Deregulations are necessary in order to simplify administration. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would like to doubt that it is really necessary for the Federal Ministry of Public Performance and Sport to decide whether to conduct degrees of service. The doctors of the Pension Insurance Institute will also examine the self-employed if they wish to move into the occupational incapacity pension, into the invalidity pension, and then determine whether there is an incapacity to serve. This is the production of equality and the elimination of injustices. I mean, this deregulation law means improvements and leads to a substantial reduction of unnecessary administration. It is a contribution to the administrative reform in Austria.

On the one hand, he said that it was unconstitutional – which is not right – and on the other, he explained that the SPÖ had been ready for discussion all the time. I must say that we had talks, but at that time Mr Wittmann was not our interlocutor, but Mr Kostelka. This matter has been moving for two years. We were very ready to talk from the beginning, and we wanted to make a four-party request and an agreement, but we did not succeed. Why didn't we do that? – Because the SPÖ has made a completely inadequate junk animation. It has injunctified the mass proceedings at the VfGH, at the Constitutional Court, with a provision according to which laws which have already been repealed must be declared unconstitutional. Only if one had complied with this request would the SPÖ have agreed that there should be discharges in the mass proceedings for the Administrative Court and for the Constitutional Court. That is, it is not at all right that a "punishment" of the VfGH should be done, as Mr Wittmann has done here – completely incorrect! – but the SPÖ itself has actually prevented possible agreement. I regret that. With regard to the so-called "unconstitutional illegality" which the SPÖ is here, I can only say that in the Committee, by the Head of the Constitutional Service and also before, in discussions with the Presidents of the Administrative Court and the Constitutional Court, and also in a review procedure, in the Committee opinion procedure, there was no record that this could be unconstitutional. Therefore, this statement of the SPÖ is very transparent. It merely serves to further postpone the possibility of mass proceedings which is very necessary for the Administrative Court. In other words, the SPÖ was in no way interested in an appropriate solution, and I regret that very much, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] They have injunctified a provision that does not have a material link with the mass procedure, which does not bring any legal improvement to citizens in particular, and does not serve to improve quality, but which is merely a means of party-political hack or serves to make small parties policy. – We reject that! On the new administrative procedure: Nor does Mr Wittmann have any right to do so, because this administrative procedure, which is intended to raise the limits on penalties and anonymity, is a classic means of administrative simplification and administrative reform. This Federal Government has taken action to simplify and streamline the administration, and we should actually support it. Member Wittmann hereby agitates that this is a pure money acquisition action and a pick-up in great manner. [[Abg. Eder: Is it!]] Mr Wittmann. These are hard words for something that is actually not right. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Young.]] – You’re right, you shouldn’t take him so seriously. I really regret that you are not involved in the objective of administrative simplification which you have already referred to as the Secretary of State, but, on the contrary, in every step that this government is taking in order to get closer to the objective of administrative simplification, to interact and to counteract with anxiety slogans and fears. We will be happy to give our consent. However, we have also stated in a committee that this is merely a measure of administrative simplification and that we assume that the individual penalties are not increased. Thank you.

For the federal government, a decrease in ordinary procedures is expected to be 3 percent. This calculated potential for savings already takes into account that an average opposition rate of 15 percent is expected. It is likely that this saving rate will increase slightly due to the expanded scope of application, but this will nevertheless lead to significant savings for the administrative authorities. This amendment does not, however, increase the fines, as the previous speakers of the opposition have constantly claimed, but merely an extended scope of this shortened procedure. This not only relieves the administrative authorities of the federal, state and local authorities, but also the citizens, for they are given the opportunity to deal with administrative violations quickly and unbureaucratically. Ladies and gentlemen! With this novel, this Federal Government continues the course of implementing a slender, civil administration in Austria. Thank you.
